How Much Are Delta SkyMiles Worth Compared To American AAdvantage Miles In 2026?

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

MC MEDIASTUDIO & Karolis Kavolelis | Shutterstock, Simple Flying

It can be difficult for airlines to truly distinguish themselves because all carriers fundamentally sell the same product: flights. Therefore, airlines need to find ways to differentiate the experience of flying from point A to point B, and that's where loyalty programs come in. By rewarding high-value customers with perks and benefits, airlines can essentially keep customers tied to their network and larger frequent flyer ecosystem.

This is an incredibly lucrative side of the business. Within the US, American Airlines' AAdvantage program was previously the US's largest airline loyalty program, but it has been overtaken by Delta Air Lines' SkyMiles. The two programs, while being similar in many ways, also have a variety of differences.

These may make one or the other more appealing to a specific customer. What's more, the individual value of a mile issued by these two programs is not necessarily the same. It's therefore important to know what the value proposition for each program is and which one is best suited for you.

The true value of airline miles is not a set figure, because this can change based on individual redemptions. However, it is possible to estimate mile valuations at a specific point in time based on systemwide redemption prices. NerdWallet estimates the value of one AAdvantage mile at 1.7 cents, while estimating that one SkyMile is worth 1.2 cents.

Meanwhile, The Points Guy estimates that one AAdvantage mile is worth 1.45 cents, while pegging the value of one SkyMile at 1.2 cents. Across the board, AAdvantage miles are considered to be more valuable than SkyMiles, and this is a unique advantage for American. NerdWallet pegs a United Airlines MileagePlus mile at 1.2 cents, while The Points Guy values MileagePlus miles the same.

There has been criticism of Delta and United for the devaluation of their programs' miles, and AAdvantage offers a material advantage in terms of redemption values. This is important when trying to decide which airline to be loyal to. At all three airlines, you can earn miles by flying or through credit card spending, which has become the primary means of mileage earnings in the US.

The airlines offer a wide range of credit cards, each coming with progressively higher annual fees and increasingly lucrative benefits. In addition, all three carriers are part of airline alliances, which grant certain benefits when traveling with select airlines worldwide, and they also have individual partnerships with airlines outside of the alliances.

Delta Air Lines uses four main Medallion tiers, as well as the general membership status and the invite-only Delta 360 tier at the very top. The four Medallion tiers, named Silver, Gold, Platinum, and Diamond, offer increasing rewards with each higher tier. In order, they respectively require 5,000, 10,000, 15,000, and 28,000 Medallion Qualifying Dollars.

These can be earned through flying or through credit card spending, with Delta not requiring members to actually fly for Medallion status. Similarly, American Airlines has its general membership tier, its invite-only ConciergeKey status, and four main elite status tiers: Gold, Platinum, Platinum Pro, and Executive Platinum. It uses Loyalty Points as the metric for status, requiring 40,000, 75,000, 125,000, and 200,000 Loyalty Points for each tier.

Crucially, however, it's much easier to earn Loyalty Points with American than MQDs with Delta, as the airline essentially awards one Loyalty Point for every eligible base mile earned. Delta awards one MQD for every dollar spent on the base ticket price and associated airline fees on Delta-operated flights, as well as bookings with Delta Vacations.

In addition, flights operated by close Delta partners earn MQDs based on a calculation of fare and distance, while SkyMiles co-branded credit cards use their own calculation: the SkyMiles Platinum and Business Platinum cards earn $1 MQD per $20 spent, while the Reserve and Business Reserve seats earn $1 MQD for every $10 spent.

SkyMiles and AAdvantage both offer four personal credit cards, with American Express issuing the SkyMiles co-branded credit cards while Citi issues the AAdvantage co-branded cards. The top-end AAdvantage Executive World Legend Mastercard notably provides unlimited Admirals Club access, whereas the top-end Delta SkyMiles Reserve Amex only grants 15 Sky Club visits annually unless the cardholder reaches $75,000 in annual spend.

This is a significant difference. As previously mentioned, the AAdvantage cards provide a quicker path to earning elite status. Both sets of cards offer a variety of benefits, and the choice of which one to choose is largely down to personal preference. However, the benefits of going with SkyMiles are that miles never expire, whereas AAdvantage miles expire after 24 months of account activity.

In addition, AAdvantage only offers one business card, whereas SkyMiles offers three, making the latter a better choice for this demographic. The AAdvantage Platinum Select World Elite Mastercard has a lower annual fee than the comparable SkyMiles Gold Amex ($99 versus $150).

Meanwhile, the AAdvantage Executive World Legend Mastercard has a higher annual fee than the SkyMiles Reserve Amex ($695 versus $650). The two airlines offer sign-on bonuses, usually in the form of bonus miles (although they can include more). Both sets of cards also grant status boosts in the form of extra Loyalty Points/MQDs in some cases, although it's still easier to earn AAdvantage Loyalty Points.

American Airlines offers its members multiple additional perks for reaching a certain amount of Loyalty Points. This is solely tied to the number of Loyalty Points reached in a calendar year, with the lowest milestone being 15,000 Loyalty Points. There are also multiple checkpoints, each offering a small variety of benefits that are different from the other milestones.

As a whole, the benefits list is quite diverse, allowing AAdvantage members across the board to receive multiple perks throughout the year. Delta Air Lines also offers SkyMiles members additional perks under the 'Choice Rewards' system. However, these are only available to Platinum and Diamond members.

Still, the rewards list is extensive for both tiers, with Platinum Medallion members able to choose options such as regional upgrade certificates, an MQD accelerator, bonus miles, a charitable donation, a status gift, statement credit, Starbucks Rewards credit, Delta Vacations credit, a Sustainable Aviation Fuel credit, or a Wheels Up credit. Diamond members get even more choices.

Delta is a SkyTeam member, and the SkyMiles program provides reciprocal SkyTeam status for the appropriate tier: SkyTeam Elite for Silver Medallion, and SkyTeam Elite Plus for the higher tiers. American Airlines, on the other hand, is a oneworld member, and oneworld offers three status tiers for loyalty program members.

The Emerald tier slots above anything that SkyTeam or Star Alliance offers in terms of benefits. This is a major incentive for AAdvantage members to chase the program's top two tiers, which grant oneworld Emerald status. SkyMiles does not publish an awards chart, as all award flights are priced dynamically, and American AAdvantage also does this for the most part.

However, AAdvantage does still use an awards chart for partner-operated flights, publishing estimated starting rates by region. What's more, American allows for upgrades from economy straight to business class (excluding basic economy fares), whereas Delta generally only permits upgrades to the next cabin class.

Multiple Delta Air Lines credit cards will help you earn elite status.

For some, there are benefits that make SkyMiles a lucrative program, such as miles that never expire, or the wider range of business credit cards. On the surface, however, there are several benefits to going for AAdvantage over SkyMiles, such as the greater upgrade flexibility, oneworld Emerald status, Admirals Club access, the Loyalty Points system, and the generally superior value per mile.

Still, two important considerations remain, which might triumph over everything else. The first is that passengers generally consider Delta to offer a better passenger experience. Although the Sky Clubs are harder to access, they're generally considered to be nicer, while nearly all Delta narrowbodies offer seatback entertainment.

SkyMiles members aren't necessarily choosing the program over competitors because of the merits of the program, but rather because they want to travel with Delta, and this may be more important to some than certain benefits offered by AAdvantage. However, arguably the most important consideration is location.

Regardless of which program's perks are more valuable, this matters little if someone lives in Atlanta, a major Delta hub, or Dallas/Fort Worth, a major American hub. The network and schedules that the dominant airline in a fortress hub offers usually have a much greater effect on which loyalty program to join. Some airports are more competitive, including smaller spokes as well as hubs like New York or Los Angeles, but even then, network and schedule play a major role.

Fortnite has begun the first Treasure Hours of the season, running from now until 4pm ET (7–9pm GMT).

During this window, everyone will drop with a Shock Rocket and Self-Revive, making for an even more chaotic Pirate Shop token hunt. With that being said, it's unclear right now if the odds of finding a token will increase during Treasure Hours.

Pirate Shop tokens make everything in the Item Shop free for two weeks.

Epic Games has only confirmed that you can expect the following:

If you miss out on the first two-hour window, Treasure Hours will return later tonight from 9-11pm ET (2–4am GMT).

An Epic Games representative declined to explain the odds of finding a Pirate Shop token to TheGamer, reiterating that everyone has an equal opportunity.

However, popular dataminer @Llamalaxy made an interesting discovery last night. Allegedly, out of the 16 possible prizes, Pirate Shop Access is the only one set to 'PreRollOnly', which means that tokens are the only prize "determined exclusively before the chest opens."

Fortnite players are already getting fed up hunting for Pirate Shop tokens, and are calling on Epic Games to disclose the odds.

Dataminers have also uncovered something called 'Clover', which handles prizes on Fortnite's servers, rather than locally. This might explain why the community has been unable to uncover the drop rates for Pirate Shop tokens.

All of which has raised questions about whether grinding for Pirate Shop tokens is even worthwhile. With the actual distribution handled server-side, players have no way of knowing whether opening more chests improves their chances.

In so many words, nobody knows what the odds are, how they're decided, or if there's a limited amount of tokens. The only detail Epic has provided is that everyone has an equal opportunity to find them.

So, for the next two hours, you can continue grinding for Pirate Shop tokens, but it's unclear right now if the Treasure Hours will make a difference, or how it's determined who gets them.

Epic’s big Item Shop gamble seems to have already paid off, with players flocking to get into Fortnite's new mini-season.

Dropout’s Vic Michaelis Wants to Toss Their Star Wars Character Out a Window

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Michaelis' Runa is the breakout star of Zero Company

Dropout star Vic Michaelis used to fall asleep to Attack of the Clones. Not because they hated it, like so many Star Wars prequel detractors, but because it was their comfort watch. “I've seen the first 45 minutes of Episode II, I want to say, 300 times,” Michaelis told Polygon. “It's so comforting. The prequels hold a place in my heart where it is like nothing bad can happen when I'm watching them.”

Michaelis has now gone from watching Star Wars to being a part of the larger Star Wars universe. In Star Wars Zero Company, they played breakout character Runa Blask, the wise-cracking advisor to the titular mercenary group, led by Captain Hawks.

Michaelis hails from the improv comedy world, but they’ve done film and scripted TV shows as well. Zero Company was their first-ever video game role. “I was so simultaneously terrified, obviously, of the video game aspect of it and also, you know, the Star Wars of it all.”

Turns out, that fear was unnecessary. The team at Bit Reactor was familiar with Michaelis’ Dropout work, and that was part of the reason they wanted Michaelis for the role.

Their improv background helped influence their performance, though they weren’t changing lines or jokes. “The improv is so much about the preparation in a way that I think people don't really know.” Instead, all of Runa’s best quips were already in the very strong script. “All of my favorite lines that Runa had were written or pitched lines from the writers,” Michaelis said.

Among those lines were many instances of Runa saying “wizard,” which basically means “cool” in Star Wars. “Runa's very tall, everything is ‘wizard,’” Michaelis said with a laugh, joking they must have said the word “500 times.”

Fans have glommed onto Runa for her constant jokes and ribbing of Hawks. “I'm seeing a lot of people being like, ‘Runa, I love her, and I wanna throw her out a window.’ Which I really resonate with, and I think was part of what we were going for,” Michaelis said. “Big sister vibes.”

Runa can’t be thrown out a window. She actually can’t be harmed at all. While the game features a permadeath feature for its operators, Runa doesn’t go on combat missions and doesn’t leave The Den, Zero Company’s headquarters.

“I did start seeing people devastated and heartbroken that some characters that they put a lot of time and energy into didn't make it through,” Michaelis said. “Do I wish people sort of mourned me in that way? Maybe. I don't know. I’m going to be thinking about that.”

Before Runa was a member of Zero Company, she was a self-described “freelance consultant to the top corporations and families in the galaxy.” AKA, a Separatist financial fixer. She desires a fortune that working for Zero Company will never grant her. As she tells Hawks, if she had that fortune, she’d want to buy a moon and run a resort. Michaelis has much humbler aspirations if they were to live in the world of Star Wars. “I would want to be a small business owner,” Michaelis said, and started describing what would make for a very fun Disney Plus show.

“I want to do a comedy night at my bookstore and all of a sudden, you've got a bunch of interplanetary politics or some red tape of why I can't do this. Then I'm marching up to the Republic with my civil city ordinances, trying to get this comedy night happening, and I'm accidentally now involved and implicated in a sort of a greater plot,” Michaelis said. “And all I wanted to do was try and get a couple of standups at my bookstore at night so I could sell wine after hours.”

If Star Trek can have a critically acclaimed animated sitcom, why can’t Star Wars have a series about a store owner just trying to get a comedy night off the ground? Your move, Disney.

Bit Reactor wanted a memorable Order 66 scene from the outset

He played for Wolves and Birmingham City - Then police made shocking discovery worth £160k

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Bethany Patrick is a football writer based in Manchester, UK.

Her work focuses on the EFL, with particular interest in football governance, supporter culture, and fan experience and access.

Bethany is a director of the award-winning Sheffield Wednesday Women’s Supporters Group, where she works on fan engagement, strategy and campaigns for greater inclusion across the game.

She regularly represents supporters in governance spaces across Europe, including government panels and discussions around football regulation and the supporter's voice.

She has previously worked as a researcher for The Stretford Paddock and is a regular contributor to The Wednesday Week.

Michael Branch was once regarded as one of English football's brightest young prospects, earning comparisons with Robbie Fowler after breaking into Everton's first team as a teenager.

The former striker went on to represent Wolverhampton Wanderers, Birmingham City and Manchester City, as well as earning international recognition with England Under-21s.

However, his promising career eventually took a dramatic turn, culminating in a seven-year prison sentence in 2012 after police uncovered his involvement in serious drug offences.

Born in Liverpool in October 1978, Branch came through Everton's academy before making his senior debut against Manchester United at Old Trafford in February 1996, aged just 17.

His emergence generated considerable excitement at Goodison Park, with the young forward quickly attracting comparisons to Liverpool striker Robbie Fowler.

However, Branch struggled to fulfil the expectations placed upon him and gradually fell out of favour at Everton.

Loan spells at Manchester City and Birmingham City followed before he joined Wolves on loan in late 1999. After scoring four times in 11 appearances, the Midlands club made his move permanent for £500,000.

Branch initially featured regularly at Molineux but struggled to maintain his form, managing just six more goals in 67 appearances following his permanent arrival.

Further loan spells at Reading and Hull City followed before he departed Wolves in 2003, subsequently representing Bradford City and Chester City.

His professional career eventually came to an end in 2006, with spells at non-league Halifax Town and Burscough following as he attempted to prolong his playing days.

Following his retirement from professional football, Branch found himself struggling with financial difficulties and became involved in the criminal underworld.

On July 10, 2012, he was arrested by officers from the Serious Organised Crime Agency following an investigation into drug supply.

The investigation uncovered two separate incidents. In March 2012, Branch had handed over almost three kilograms of amphetamine to another man in a Liverpool car park.

Andre has revealed he turned down 'life-changing money' from Liverpool when he was at Fluminense, a year before he left to join Wolves.

Four months later, officers searched his home in Otterspool, Liverpool, where they discovered one kilogram of high-purity cocaine.

The drugs were reported to have an estimated street value of approximately £160,000, making for a shocking discovery involving a former professional footballer who had once represented his country at youth level.

Branch subsequently admitted charges of supplying Class A and Class B drugs at Chester Crown Court. On November 8, 2012, he was sentenced to seven years in prison.

He ultimately served three-and-a-half years behind bars, with his release coming in 2016.

However, Branch's story did not end with his imprisonment, with the former Everton forward working hard to rebuild his life following his release.

He joined Everton in the Community in 2016, initially working on programmes supporting former offenders as they attempted to reintegrate into society.

His experiences also helped him connect with vulnerable young people.

Branch has since taken on various roles within the charity, including outreach work supporting young people into education, training and employment.

More recently, he established Social Striders Liverpool, a free running group designed to promote positive mental health through exercise and conversation.

Having previously spoken openly about the difficulties he experienced following his imprisonment, Branch has also become an advocate for mental health awareness and rehabilitation.

Chauffeur Life

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

My guests’ expertise in rear-seat amenities provides some insight into passenger-pleasing design.

The kids sure like to drink. Just not alcohol. Data shows that’s down by a considerable percentage among young people. Good for them. No, the youths in my social circle like to stay hydrated. They are never without a water bottle (bedazzled or stickered to set it apart from everyone else’s Owalas and Stanleys) or a sweet tea with a fat straw from a boba shop.

On a recent weekend visit from my college bestie and her teen and tween daughters, we must have hit every bubble-beverage and smoothie spot in town. All of that chauffeuring made me hyperaware of what makes for a well-appointed back seat. Number one, according to my expert passengers? Cupholders. Not just for drinks, but also for phones and sunglasses, because it’s easier to remember them when they’re sticking up rather than hiding inside a console or lying on a charging pad. Note to designers: Can you do phone-charging cupholders?

The Ford Ranger that I had for the weekend got high marks for its back-seat cupholders and the fold-down console with its padded armrest. “I hate when there’s nowhere to lean,” declared the 16-year-old, weary already. It’s exhausting being so cool.

See all results for used cars for sale near 90017

Complaints about the Ranger were the lack of dedicated climate vents for the back seat—their mother and I were freezing up front while they were still too warm in the back—and the hefty step-up into the cabin, which was challenging while carrying a large milk tea with mango jelly. “I can’t hold the door and jump down at the same time,” the younger one said, exhibiting a remarkable concern for parking-lot door dings rarely shown by people who don’t pay insurance premiums (and often not even by those who do).

A later ride in my ’80s Dodge Ramcharger had the same step-up and A/C problems, but the couchlike bench seat and full-length windows offset the lack of modern amenities. That vehicle is a favorite of my neighbor’s kids too, so if you’re investing in collector cars, grab a Ramcharger or a Plymouth Trail Duster before they go the way of vintage Broncos in price.

While trucks from that era might seem like a potential family travel solution, not everyone is willing to let their kids ride in a vehicle where the closest things to airbags are the Gabriel HiJackers someone installed in 1973 and the whoopee cushion that’s been under the seat since two owners ago. With that in mind, I made a list of some modern back-seat luxuries that have impressed me or my discerning young friends in recent new cars.

Dual Bluetooth connectivity: Unless you like being critiqued for your playlist, the ideal setup lets a back-seat passenger connect their phone for media while the driver links theirs for ­navigation­ and­ communication.­ I ­first ­noticed­ this ­feature ­on a Dodge Durango, but it’s increasingly common in newer infotainment systems.

See all results for used cars for sale near 90017

Third-row USB ports: It’s pretty much a given that second rows will have charging points, but with bigger families, that middle row is where the child safety seats latch in. While little ones might run low on apple juice, the older crew in the wayback will require a different kind of recharge. If you’re looking at base models, make sure to check the USB-port placement, as those are the trims most likely to cheap out on the third row.

Smart seats: I recently drove the new long-wheelbase Tesla Model Y L­ and­ noted that ­the­ middle-row­ chairs­ fold­ flat­ but­ don’t ­slide.­ While­ I wasn’t thrilled about having to make my way down the center aisle, airplane style, to get to the third row, I was impressed that the seatbacks in the second and third rows can be raised or lowered from the driver’s seat. Another neat trick is that if the front passenger’s seat is left too far back, it can adjust itself to prevent the middle row from crashing into it during folding. Also, the middle-row armrests automatically lowered when the door opened to make entry to the back easier. This is what I want from technology: smart seats. Now give me back a physical shifter.

Sparkle: At the end of the weekend, I asked the 11-year-old her thoughts on the Ranger. “It’s okay,” she said. “Only I like those cars where the tables fold out of the seats and the ceiling is all sparkly, like those caves where there are stars in the cave.” Rolls-Royces? I also like those cars. I respect her outlook, so I didn’t tell her what those cave stars actually are. Note to designers: Have you considered glowworms?

Like a sleeper agent activated late in the game, Elana Scherr didn’t know her calling at a young age. Like many girls, she planned to be a vet-astronaut-artist, and came closest to that last one by attending UCLA art school. She painted images of cars, but did not own one. Elana reluctantly got a driver’s license at age 21 and discovered that she not only loved cars and wanted to drive them, but that other people loved cars and wanted to read about them, which meant somebody had to write about them. Since receiving activation codes, Elana has written for numerous car magazines and websites, covering classics, car culture, technology, motorsports, and new-car reviews. In 2020, she received a Best Feature award from the Motor Press Guild for the C/D story "A Drive through Classic Americana in a Polestar 2."  In 2023, her Car and Driver feature story "In Washington, D.C.'s Secret Carpool Cabal, It's a Daily Slug Fest" was awarded 1st place in the 16th Annual National Arts & Entertainment Journalism Awards by the Los Angeles Press Club.

TeraWulf Says AI Leasing Now Generates 83% of Revenue as Bitcoin Mining Recedes

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: TeraWulf’s November 6 earnings release shows $63 million in third-quarter high-performance-computing lease revenue, around 83% of its $76.2 million total. The company is increasingly an AI infrastructure business, although large non-cash gains and long-dated construction plans require careful reading.

TeraWulf is becoming a clear example of how the economics of Bitcoin mining can lead an infrastructure operator into a rather different business. In its third-quarter results published November 6, the Nasdaq-listed company said high-performance computing, or HPC, generated $63 million of lease revenue in the three months to September 30.

That represented about 83% of its $76.2 million quarterly revenue. Investors who still describe the firm primarily as a Bitcoin miner risk missing where its business is now concentrated and what its future costs may look like.

The company operates existing mining infrastructure at its Lake Mariner campus but is repurposing portions of that site to support contracted computing workloads. TeraWulf said 102 megawatts of revenue-generating critical IT capacity were online at Lake Mariner at quarter-end, with another 336 megawatts under construction.

These numbers are not interchangeable with power agreements or theoretical campus capacity. A contract for future electricity or land does not mean an equivalent amount of paying data-centre capacity is already operating. The distinction matters when a company is projecting revenue from facilities that require years and significant capital to complete.

TeraWulf also reported net income of approximately $709.9 million for the quarter. But it attributed a significant benefit to a non-cash change in the fair value of warrants, while adjusted EBITDA was negative at about $12.8 million. That makes the headline profit a poor substitute for analysing recurring rental revenue, financing costs and operating cash flow.

The company’s release described a 20-year Anthropic lease covering approximately 401 megawatts at its Justified development in Kentucky, representing around $19 billion in contracted revenue over the initial term. It also described a Google credit backstop supporting anticipated project financing and a definitive engineering, procurement and construction agreement with Fluor. These are material commercial commitments, not a claim that $19 billion has already been received.

TeraWulf expects phased service availability at Justified to begin in late 2027 and continue into 2028. At Muskie, it said contracted power capacity had expanded toward one gigawatt, subject to regulatory approval. Schedules, financing and utility infrastructure remain central risks.

NewsBTC has previously examined the mining industry’s move towards AI infrastructure. TeraWulf’s fresh results make that theme measurable: a majority of present revenue now comes from leases rather than mining.

A miner switching to AI can reduce its exposure to Bitcoin production economics, including changes in difficulty, coin price and power cost. It can also create demand for the same grid connections, facilities and financing that historically supported mining. That transition need not be immediately positive or negative for Bitcoin’s network; it depends on which machines are retired, which are redeployed and whether remaining miners take up capacity.

The contrast with NewsBTC’s discussion of rising network hashrate is telling. Competition for mining rewards can intensify even while some large listed companies reshape themselves into computing landlords.

The milestones to watch are completed megawatts, actual rental commencement, capital spending against guidance, contractual counterparty exposure and cash generation. TeraWulf’s results show a dramatic change in revenue mix, but the hardest part of its next chapter will be delivering enormous physical projects reliably and profitably.

Gilligan's Island Star Jim Backus Voiced One Of Animation's Most Famous Characters

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Though he may not be a household name today, Jim Backus was a well known character actor in the mid-20th Century. Of course, his most recognizable role will forever remain Thurston Howell III from "Gilligan's Island," as Backus brought a suitably refined energy to the part of a New England patrician stranded alongside other societal archetypes in the South Pacific. But Backus' career stretched far beyond three seasons of a beloved CBS sitcom. Both prior to and after becoming trapped on the Isle, for instance, he voiced the animated character Quincy Magoo, better known as Mr. Magoo — a role he played from 1949 until 1977.

The actor's pre-Thurston Howell career was already impressive. Before "Gilligan's Island," Backus appeared opposite Clint Eastwood in the movie "Francis in the Navy," although he gained far more in the way of significant recognition for putting on an acting masterclass as the Stark family patriarch, Frank Stark, in the James Dean classic "Rebel Without a Cause." That likely would've gone down as his biggest non-"Gilligan's Island" role, too, if it weren't for the fact he voiced Mr. Magoo for some three decades.

Like Thurston Howell, Mr. Magoo was a millionaire. Unlike Thurston Howell, however, this deep-pocketed, diminutive magnate had a severe case of near-sightedness — a condition he refused to acknowledge. Created by Millard Kaufman and John Hubley for the animation studio United Productions of America, Mr. Magoo first appeared in the 1949 theatrical short "The Ragtime Bear," which became a hit and ensured the character would stick around for a truly incredible amount of time. Indeed, the most recent "Mr. Magoo" cartoon ended in 2023, well after Backus had passed away. Still, he did voice the character for his first 30-ish years, arguably making Mr. Magoo Backus' most important role.

In a piece for Cinema Journal title "Memories of Mr. Magoo" (via JSTOR), producer Howard Rieder recalled how, long before Jim Backus' casting caused rewrites on "Gilligan's Island," the actor came to be hired for "The Ragtime Bear." It seems co-creator John Hubley asked actor Jerry Hausner, who voiced the character of Mr. Magoo's nephew and frequent sidekick Waldo, to help him find a suitable star. According to Rieder, Hausner remembered that Backus had arrived from New York where he was voicing the haughty playboy Hubert Updike III as part of the radio series "The Alan Young Show." It seems the actor was playing snooty millionaires long before he set foot on Gilligan's Isle.

According to Hausner's recollection, Backus was "fairly well-known" as the voice of Updike, and he seemed perfect for the voice of Mr. Magoo. "We had been friends for over 20 years," recalled Hausner (via Rieder). "I spoke to Hubley about Backus, and he wanted to meet him." Evidently, Backus was already well-established by this point, and Hubley didn't want to make him audition. Instead, he took him to the UPA studios and showed him character designs and storyboards. There, Backus found a personal connection to the character. As Hausner explained:

"He said, 'My father lives in his own little world, never quite seeing things the way they really are. It isn't that he's nearsighted, but his whole attitude toward life is a kind of personal isolation toward the rest of the world.' Backus mentioned that he used to do a character called the Man in the Club Car. It was the character of the businessman, the tycoon, the loudmouth talker [...] He began to use this voice and it was the voice that ultimately became Mr. Magoo."

After making his debut as Mr. Magoo in "The Ragtime Bear," Jim Backus went on to voice the character in multiple theatrical cartoons and the 1960s syndicated TV series "Mister Magoo." He returned to voice the character in multiple TV specials, along with two follow-up series: "The Famous Adventures of Mr. Magoo," which aired between 1964 and '65, and "What's New, Mr. Magoo?" in 1977.

Over the course of his multiple films and series, the character evolved from a mean-spirited, irascible toff to a senescent, lovable buffoon. The comedy arose from his inability to recognize the often precarious and dangerous situations into which he frequently stumbled due to his near-sightedness. And while those around him were perpetually stressed by his near-misses, due to sheer luck, Mr. Magoo always seemed to come out unscathed.

Backus gave voice to this miraculously lucky millionaire right up until the end of "What's New, Mr. Magoo?" in 1977 — the year before he returned as Thurston Howell III for one of the three "Gilligan's Island" TV movies, "Rescue from Gilligan's Island." Unfortunately, the actor was in bad health by the time he made a brief cameo appearance in 1981's "The Harlem Globetrotters on Gilligan's Island," and he passed away in 1989.

Mr. Magoo, however, endured. Not only did the Backus-voiced version remain part of popular culture via syndication deals, Leslie Nielsen played the character in an ill-fated 1997 live-action film that became a box office disappointment and debuted to negative reviews. More recently, a French animated series, "Mr. Magoo," began airing in 2019 and lasted until 2023.

→ Baca artikel asli

Keira Knightley and ‘Black Doves’ Creator Joe Barton Why Helen Should Have Been the One to Kill [SPOILER], That Oner and Hopes for Season 3

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

SPOILER ALERT: This article contains major spoilers for “Black Doves” Season 2, now streaming on Netflix.

Keira Knightley is back as super spy Helen Webb in Season 2 of “Black Doves,” alongside Ben Whishaw as her troubled colleague Sam Young and Sarah Lancashire as her stoic spymaster Reed.

The show, created by Joe Barton, returns with plenty of action, blood and bodies as Reed goes missing, Sam makes a Faustian pact with a shady benefactor and Helen’s secret identity is almost exposed — a threat that’s all the more serious given her new role in Number 10 Downing Street now that her husband, Wallace (Andrew Buchan), has become Prime Minister.

With Season 2 out now on Netflix, Knightley and Barton sat down with Variety to discuss why the showrunner chose to focus on Reed’s backstory this season, how the series differs from Apple TV’s London spy thriller “Slow Horses” — and the one character Knightley really wanted to kill.

Joe Barton: It organically came out the storytelling process of Reed’s backstory, and the idea of the sins of the past coming forward and everyone suffering for them. And I wanted to do a “Berlin in the ‘80s” spy thing.

Keira Knightley: I was gonna say, I think you’ve got a front row seat to that one.

Knightley: [Joe] handed me the script that [said] “You’re shooting the nanny,” and I was like, “But she stays late, what the fuck are you talking about? You don’t shoot her! She never goes home — this is the childcare of dreams.” But she has taken the kids, so we do have to kill the nanny. So we did have that conversation.

Knightley: That’s what I love about it, because life doesn’t stop just because you’ve got other stuff. I think that’s what’s lovely about the whole show, is that as heightened and — I mean this in the best way — ridiculous as the whole thing is, it equally has that very human part of it, and that’s what makes it great, in my opinion. That’s what makes it really fun, and that’s what makes it really interesting.

Barton: She doesn’t have it [in real life].

Knightley: Oh yeah, you did actually do that research.

Barton: The prime minister’s wife doesn’t get a security detail, so it’s very realistic.

Knightley: She’s got a hat and glasses, come on. It’s fine!

Knightley: [To Barton] How much are we balancing reality?

Barton: Well, it’s a tricky balancing act, isn’t it?

Barton: I would say we’re not always balancing it.

Knightley: It’s a foot off the floor. A good foot off the floor, sometimes two feet off the floor.

Barton: A couple of meters off the floor! We get talked about a lot with “Slow Horses,” because obviously [they’re both] London spy shows. I think the big difference between us and them is ostensibly “Slow Horses” takes place in the real world, and I don’t think we do. There are obviously recognizable elements of the real world, but fundamentally it’s more me taking a chapter out of the James Bond or “Mission Impossible” book, where you can have these almost flights of fantasy. We have our cake and eat it too, basically.

Barton: With the prime minister’s wife, my argument is always I don’t actually know what Keir Starmer’s wife looks like.

Knightley: When we were shooting, it was Keir Starmer’s wife [Starmer resigned in June], and we were all like, “Actually, I don’t know her name particularly, and I don’t know what she looks like.” So we were all very pleased about that, because we were like, “See? She could be doing anything.”

Knightley: I didn’t get to, no — he made me shoot the nanny instead! I was like, “That’s not the one I want to kill. I want to kill Dani! Why didn’t you let me kill Dani? You killed Dani, and you didn’t let me do it.”

Barton: Do you know what that was? I wanted to keep Dani alive so that you could kill her, [but] it didn’t make sense to get your character there. But then I was on set with Aggie and we were talking. I was like, “I love working with you, and I think the character is so interesting and there could be more.” And she’s like, “Oh no, the thing is, I’ve just got this new job, so I wouldn’t even be available for another series.”

Barton: I just thought it would be so shocking to do that to poor Neve.

Knightley: I didn’t see that coming. Another one bites the dust.

Knightley: We didn’t have a lot of time to rehearse that sequence, and all of a sudden, Julian [Farino, the director] said, “Actually, I want to shoot it in a oner.” And me and Ambika were like, “But we haven’t learnt the fight, so how are you going to shoot it in a oner?” And [the crew] were like, “Well — good luck! We’ll just try and make it work!” So it was quite an interesting day. We did actually manage to shoot it in a oner.

Barton: It ended up working really well, but it wasn’t always a guarantee, was it?

Knightley: It did end up working out well. It definitely didn’t feel like a guarantee on the day that it would.

Barton: Yeah, we have bits of Christmas. We pop in and out of it.

Knightley: We were shooting over Christmas again, which meant that it was quite hard to avoid at certain points. But actually, when we finally shot in Trafalgar Square, it was post-Christmas, so we did then have to invent the Christmas tree.

Barton: We had to digitally put the tree in. The first [season] we were chasing Christmas, and this one we were running from it. The [real] tree is quite threadbare, so we were like “We could make it even nicer,” and the Norwegian government [who give the Trafalgar Square Christmas tree to the U.K. each year] were like “No, you’re not allowed to, it has to look like [the real thing].”

Barton: Yeah it has to be the same type of tree and you’re not allowed to make it too –

Barton: I’m in a writers’ room for it at the moment, and we’re hoping.

This interview has been edited and condensed.

Parents want safer phones for kids. These companies are answering the call.

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Buy One Founder Summit Ticket, Get a Second for Free. Register now.

Founder Summit begins Nov. 4, 9:00 a.m. ET. Get your pass savings & deals before it starts. REGISTER HERE.

As parents become increasingly concerned about the potential dangers of unrestricted smartphone access for children, a growing number of companies are developing devices specifically designed for kids.

Today’s kid-friendly phones typically feature touchscreens, cameras, and communication capabilities similar to regular smartphones. However, the software on these devices is heavily modified to remove or restrict access to web browsers, social media, app stores, and other distractions.

Parents can usually manage these devices through a companion app, allowing them to approve contacts, monitor locations, set screen time limits, select available apps, and, in some cases, receive alerts regarding cyberbullying, explicit content, or online predators.

In addition to these modified devices, some companies offer a more minimalist approach, creating devices that focus solely on calling and texting.

There are also home phones designed specifically for children, like Tin Can and the recently launched Pinwheel Home.

Here’s a look at the major players shaping the kid-phone market.

The Bark Phone is probably one of the most popular kid-specific smartphones on the market.

Built on Samsung Galaxy hardware and powered by Bark’s monitoring software, it only allows calls and texts with approved contacts. Bark continuously scans texts, emails, photos, and supported apps for signs of cyberbullying, grooming, suicidal ideation, sexual content, and other potential concerns, then sends alerts to parents.

A big standout feature is that parents can gradually unlock web browsing, apps, and other features as their child matures. The phone also includes GPS tracking and screen-time management.

Current pricing for the standard model is $240, plus a required wireless plan beginning at $29/month. Higher-tier plans add internet access and unlimited texting.

Gabb is a well-known name in the kid-friendly phone category. Unlike Bark, however, Gabb focuses less on monitoring content and more on eliminating online risks altogether.

Its phones don’t include social media, a browser, or an app store. Instead, children get calling, texting, and a carefully curated selection of preloaded apps, including a camera, calendar, and calculator. The company also touts its “worry-free” music streaming library with millions of songs.

Additionally, parents can view location and manage certain device settings, while optional services such as “Gabb Guard” help block spam calls and unwanted texts.

Current phone pricing starts around $159.99, with cellular service starting around $24.99 per month.

With Pinwheel, parents approve every app, control contacts, schedule screen time, create custom routines, and monitor location history.

One standout feature is the ability to create different “modes” throughout the day. For example, limiting the phone to calling and navigation during school hours before unlocking additional features after homework.

Pinwheel phones start around $119, while the Caregiver Portal subscription begins at $14.99 per month without cell service. (Cellular service can be purchased separately through participating carriers.)

The company recently launched two landline phones for $68 and $79. It also offers a smartwatch at $160.

Launched in 2022, Teracube offers a tailored version of Android known as Thrive OS. This enables parents to approve every app download, filter web browsing, set app-specific and overall screen-time limits, monitor location, and create customized routines for school or bedtime. However, because it has more traditional smartphone features, it’s often recommended for older children.

Thrive currently sells for $99, with plans starting at $35 per month.

Unlike the other products in this roundup, Ooma MyPhone isn’t a mobile phone at all. This kid-focused home phone is designed as a modern replacement for the traditional household landline.

The service allows calls only between approved contacts through its Trusted Circle feature, offers scheduled Quiet Hours, provides address-based 911 service, sends emergency alerts when 911 is dialed, and lets parents review call logs through an online portal. There are no apps, internet browsing, texting, or social media.

The phone costs $99.99, while service starts at $7.99 per month.

For $100, Tin Can resembles a landline phone with a distinctive tin can design, which is the inspiration behind its name.

This device does not require a traditional phone jack, but connects via Wi-Fi. Only approved contacts can make calls, effectively preventing spam or unauthorized communications. Parents can easily manage contacts through a user-friendly companion app.

Tin Can offers a free plan for calling other Tin Can users, as well as a $9.99 per month plan that allows calls to all approved contacts.

A newer entrant in the space, Waffle recently opened preorders for its screen-free devices: Waffle Home ($119), a landline phone, and Waffle Go ($169), which looks like a portable walkie-talkie or kids’ toy with three simple buttons that can only make voice calls and listen to voice notes.

Parents can approve contacts via the companion app, as well as see location and control when devices can call.

TextNow, the Canadian telecommunications company, recently launched its new mobile phone for kids. It’s called Yello! and while it looks like a normal touchscreen smartphone, it doesn’t have an app store, social media, or unrestricted web access.

Parents can see their kids’ location and battery life, as well as control things like the games they play and who they have in their contacts.

Yello! costs $149.99 upfront, with a $19.99 per month plan.

The Velotric GoMad Electric Utility eBike — CleanTechnica Tested

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Velotric has emerged as one of the largest ebike brands in the US, offering a wide range of models for buyers to choose from. Having a wide range of models with differing capabilities is increasingly a key factor for buyers when deciding which ebike to buy, as it allows them to upgrade or swap out for a different model to accommodate different stages of life.

They reached out to CleanTechnica about a review and we were excited to play with the Velotric GoMad Electric Utility bike, because it is just so darn flexible in what it can do. You can throw a set of bags on the back rack or a passenger kit to bring a friend with you. You can put a basket on the back and deliver pizzas, or you can just throw a backpack on and ride around town solo. It truly looked like the jack of all trades, so we pulled the trigger, and after a few days, it showed up.

Disclaimer: Velotric provided the GoMad to the author for the purposes of this review.

Assembling the Velotric GoMad is similar to the process for most e-bikes. It can be done with a basic set of tools that are included with the bike, and takes between 30 minutes and an hour depending on how much of a hurry you’re in.

After that, it’s a simple matter of attaching the pedals, mounting the handlebars to the stem, installing the front wheel and tire, connecting a few of the electrical connectors between the handlebars and the bike itself, airing up the tires, and you’re on your way. Ours took a few minutes longer than normal to bolt on the optional Passenger Kit

Taking in the Velotric GoMad, it is an attractive bike with great proportions. The battery is integrated into the main downtube on the frame, which makes it look slightly bloated, but in a way that fits into the overall proportions of the bike. The oversized tires help with this, making it look like it can go absolutely anywhere.

The rear hub motor kicks out plenty of power, and with a range of pedal assist modes. You can choose just how much power you need for the ride you want. You can choose from the motor being completely off, Eco, Tour, Trail, Sport, and even the full-out Boost mode that can give you exactly the support you need.

It’s perfect for when you’re carrying a load, powering up a hill, or just feeling tired. The motor is always ready to step in to provide assistance. At the upper end of its power cycle, the motor controller is more sensitive to the pedaling cadence, resulting in the motor pulsing on and off, providing for a bit of a bumpy sensation when pedaling. It’s only something you’d notice if you’re really paying attention, but it’s there.

The GoMad is designed with an integrated rear rack and a hidden storage pocket. The rack can also be converted into a seat for a second passenger with Velotric’s passenger kit. The passenger kit itself is amazing if that’s what you’re looking for, and this is further accentuated by the bike’s compact footprint. The tradeoff is that the main seat has a flat rear, which does make it slightly less comfortable than more fully built-out options.

The passenger kit comes with a set of pegs down below for the passengers’ feet to rest on, a rear grab rail for the rack, and a front handle for the passenger to hang onto as you’re boosting around town. Given the compact footprint of the bike, it feels like it was designed to be used as more of a teenager-plus-one setup, but if you want to put it to work as a husband-and-wife date night setup, its 500 pound weight capacity is more than up to the task.

I rode around on it with my wife on the back and it was very comfortable and stable for both of us. The foot pegs and the solid support bars that come with the passenger kit make it feel much safer and more comfortable for the passenger. I took a few pics of us on the bike, but, hilariously, none of them had the bike in frame and she wasn’t a fan of my one handed stunts. Of course, that’s for you to decide what you want to do with your life and how you want to set up your bike and put it to use.

The Velotric Nomad is an extremely capable bike at a mid-level price point for bikes of this quality and capability. It’s currently listed as $1,899 with a special Black Friday offer that includes a free young passenger kit.

The fact that its 750W continuous power output motor has a peak of 1,300W means it’s got plenty of pep to take even the largest and least-in-shape riders wherever they want to go. The specs across the board and the integration of the torque and cadence sensor make it feel like a very natural ride that will surprise you with just how intuitively the power is coming from the motor.

I especially love the colorways this bike comes in. They have a nice modern-looking sage that feels classy and refined, a dark amber that’s somewhere between a bright orange and bright red that looks like a pop of lipstick that really sets the whole frame off, and finally an obsidian, which is what our review bike came in.

The obsidian has a base color that looks almost black, but then when you put it in the sunlight, it has a rainbow pop of color that peeks out every once in a while. It’s not so much that it feels like it’s covered in glitter, but it’s just enough to make it feel fun.

For more information about the Velotric Nomad e-bike, head over to their official website, where you can learn all about it or purchase one for yourself.

Sheffield United manager Chris Wilder rues 'missed opportunity' after defeat to Charlton Athletic

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Sheffield United manager Chris Wilder was left to rue a “missed opportunity” after his side were beaten 2-1 by Charlton Athletic.

The Blades had a chance to move into the Championship play-off places at The Valley but left the capital empty-handed.

Did you know with a Digital subscription to Yorkshire Post, you can get access to all of our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

Millenic Alli fired the hosts ahead after 12 minutes and Matty Godden restored the Charlton lead after Jordan Ayew’s equaliser.

Wilder said: “You look at the results after we came in and you scratch your head. But the one we have to concentrate on is our own and we have not done enough things right.

“We didn’t capitalise on our moments to get the result we were all after. I’m not going to take a wrecking ball to it.

“There is always emotion in it – when you win then you’re going up and when you don’t then it is a different narrative.

“We will keep calm, like I have all the way through. I kept calm after we beat Wrexham, Bristol City and when we won on Tuesday. There are mini setbacks, mini highs and mini lows that we need to deal with.

“It is a missed opportunity. I’d have loved to have climbed into the top seven, but we need to rest and recover over the international break.”

Wilder was particularly frustrated by the nature of Charlton’s goals, as he felt the Addicks did not have to work “unbelievably hard” to register them.

He said: “It’s been a frustrating day. They have not had to work unbelievably hard for their goals.

“We have to be stronger in the lead up to the first one. Michael [Cooper] has been outstanding and it is a difficult one because it has dipped and bounced in front of him.

“The second one is the frustrating one. I played at full-back and there are going to be times that wingers go past you.

“The distances between Liam Kitching, Mark McGuinness and Japh [Tanganga] were too big. Godden is a goal-scorer and they allow him to get in front. It gave them a huge boost and huge energy.”

Charlton boss Nathan Jones, meanwhile, said: “Relief is a big thing – emotionally – because of everything that has gone on. That was more like us from start to finish. We were front-footed and had real control in our play.

“I’m really proud of the group, because they’ve had to cope with so much noise and pressure.

“We have lost five or six people this week through injury and what not. So to respond in the manner we did, apart from Wembley it is probably my favourite result at this football club.

“It was wonderful. It’s only one win, but it’s an important one. We won’t over-egg it because it is just one win.

“The crowd have been really good with me. I probably used up all their patience in midweek. Hopefully I’ve taken one step back into credit.”

Furious Tottenham set to complain to authorities after Jayden Bogle escapes red card in Leeds' 1-0 win at Elland Road

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Published: 13:54 EDT, 7 November 2026 | Updated: 14:17 EDT, 7 November 2026

Tottenham are to issue a formal complaint over the decision not to show a red card to Leeds defender Jayden Bogle after his challenge on Mathys Tel at Elland Road.

Video replays showed Bogle catch the Spurs forward on the back of his left ankle in the first half of Saturday’s lunchtime kick off but despite a lengthy review by the VAR, referee Jon Brooks was not sent to the pitchside monitor.

Tel came back on but was replaced by Dominic Solanke after 43 minutes and Leeds went on to win the game 1-0 through a Noel Okafor goal.

Spurs, who will lodge their protest with Pro Ref, may cite the red card shown to Crystal Palace striker Jean-Philippe Mateta in the previous Saturday’s clash at the Tottenham Stadium for his foul on Micky van de Ven.

On that occasion, referee Michael Oliver originally gave a yellow card but it was upgraded after a video assistant referee review.

Former Premier League assistant referee Darren Cann told BBC that the Leeds defender was ‘fortunate’ not to be dismissed.

Tel came back on but was replaced by Dominic Solanke after 43 minutes and Leeds went on to win the game 1-0

Crystal Palace striker Jean-Philippe Mateta was sent off for this foul on Micky van de Ven in the previous Saturday’s clash

‘Bogle's challenge meets the criteria for serious foul play as he endangers the safety of an opponent,’ Cann said. ‘He can consider himself lucky to still be on the pitch.’

Tottenham manager Roberto De Zerbi was more critical of his own players afterwards, but did say of the decision: 'I have big confidence in the referee. Nothing changes if he goes to the monitor.

'If they didn't take the right decision I don't know. We have to keep the eyes on the performance where can improve.'

The decision will be reviewed by the Key Match Incidents Panel in the coming days. Mateta is serving a three-match suspension after the Football Association rejected Palace's appeal against his red card he received in their 2–0 defeat to Tottenham.

Trump offers UFC chief Dana White press secretary job

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

President Donald Trump offered UFC president and CEO Dana White the job of press secretary on Saturday.

In a post on Truth Social, Trump said: "I am pleased to announce that the Great Dana White, a violent Fighter and Warrior, has been offered the job of Press Secretary of the United States."

"We need these people to defend us against the Lunatic Left," Trump wrote. "Dana will be a Press Secretary like none other. Dana, please take the job!"

Apparently in response to Trump's post, White posted a picture of himself on Instagram stories standing behind the press secretary's lectern in the White House, with the caption, "On to the next chapter."

It was unclear whether White has accepted the position or is simply acknowledging the president's request in jest.

Trump said that he was offering White the job of "Press Secretary of the United States," but the president's spokesperson is called the "White House press secretary." Trump also recently speculated about considering Trump loyalist and rapper Nicki Minaj as White House press secretary, in an interview with podcaster Benny Johnson.

Trump said the job offer to White extended to his assistants, "even though some are not from our Country." The so-called assistants Trump named included current and former UFC mixed martial arts fighters from countries such as Brazil and Russia.

In October, White House press secretary Katie Zacharia left the press secretary position, just five days after her stint as Karoline Leavitt's replacement began.

The president told reporters he suggested Zacharia quit after her first and only press briefing, where, in her six minutes answering questions behind the lectern, she only took questions from three conservative outlets.

"I just spoke to her about it. I said, 'Katie, is this for you?'" Trump said outside the White House on Oct. 16.

The president's appeal to White follows Friday's announcement that Trump's deputy chief of staff, Stephen Miller, would be promoted to chief of staff, replacing Susie Wiles.

Trump's stunning shake-up comes just three days after Democrats won a landslide victory in the midterm elections, gaining majority control in the House and Senate.

The president and White have a longstanding friendship. During Trump's first campaign in 2016, White praised the then-Republican nominee and called him a "fighter."

"Nobody took us seriously. Nobody. Except Donald Trump. Donald was the first guy to recognize the potential we saw in the UFC and encouraged us to build our business," White said.

And for his 80th birthday in June, President Trump hosted a UFC match — dubbed UFC Freedom 250 — on the White House's South Lawn.

The White House and the UFC didn't immediately respond to a request for comment.

— CNBC's Kevin Breuninger contributed to this report.

Wall Street’s Stunning Post-Election Rebound Puts Bitcoin and Crypto Markets in Focus

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Wall Street staged a sharp recovery following the U.S. midterm elections, reversing earlier losses as investors reassessed the implications of Democrats taking control of both chambers of Congress.

Notably, the turnaround has also drawn attention to Bitcoin and the wider cryptocurrency market as traders weigh the political outcome against its potential impact on crypto regulations.

According to analyst Bull Theory, the recovery was particularly striking given the scale of the initial sell-off. NASDAQ futures had dropped more than 2% as election results pointed to a Democratic sweep, while Japan’s Nikkei and South Korea’s KOSPI also suffered significant losses.

However, major indices subsequently rebounded from their session lows. The S&P 500 recovered 2.18%, while the NASDAQ gained 3.13%. The Dow Jones and Russell 2000 advanced 2.16% and 2.31%, respectively. In Asia, Japan’s Nikkei 225 climbed 5.09%, while South Korea’s KOSPI rose 4.71%.

Bull Theory attributed the reversal partly to investors reassessing what a Democratic-controlled Congress means for President Donald Trump’s remaining two years in office.

Although Democrats are projected to control both chambers from January 3, 2027, Republicans retain control until then. Furthermore, Trump’s presidential powers remain intact, including his ability to veto legislation passed by Congress. Overriding a veto would require a two-thirds majority in both chambers, a threshold Democrats cannot reach on their own.

Notably, the election outcome could make it harder for Trump to advance his legislative agenda without necessarily allowing Democrats to push through major policy changes of their own. Investors may therefore be adjusting their expectations rather than treating the result as an immediate threat to financial markets.

Bitcoin also fell during the election-related sell-off, briefly dropping into the $75,000 range as a Democratic victory in both chambers became increasingly likely. However, the cryptocurrency recovered as uncertainty about the political outcome eased.

TDX Strategies founder Dick Lo explained that the recovery followed a reduction in election-related uncertainty.

“Bitcoin dropped initially in response to the Democratic sweep of the midterm elections, but has since recovered as a clear outcome resolves political uncertainty,” he told CoinDesk.

He added that a Democratic-controlled Congress is generally viewed as less favorable for pro-crypto legislation, while divided government makes major, market-unfriendly policy changes less likely.

Meanwhile, analytics platform 10x Research warned that Bitcoin and Ethereum remain at an important technical turning point despite easing election-related risks.

In a post on X, the firm wrote, “While our systematic trend models remain technically short both Bitcoin and Ethereum, fading consensus to trade against established signals demands exceptional risk/reward skew.“

The firm explained that traders should look beyond political developments when assessing whether the recent correction is a temporary setback in a bull market or the start of a more significant shift in market direction.

Its framework focuses on longer-term market indicators, trading-volume support levels, and key price thresholds to determine whether investors should remain defensive or consider taking bullish positions.

Nevertheless, the post-election recovery does not eliminate other risks facing digital assets. TDX Strategies pointed to recent exchange security breaches as a potential source of pressure on investor sentiment, even as declining political uncertainty creates room for market recovery.

That said, with Bitcoin and Ethereum still facing technical resistance, traders will be watching whether the broader market rebound translates into sustained buying interest across cryptocurrencies.

De Laurentiis. “Nessun caso, ho preferito bloccare la conferenza. Giochiamo ogni tre giorni”

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

A Former 1900s Lakeside Resort in Georgia Is for Sale as an $8.85 Million Private Retreat

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Owning waterfront property is one thing. Owning the entire lake is another.

The lake, created from a dam on Ichawaynochaway Creek, is just part of the 1,442-acre property in Leary, GA listed for $8.85 million.

"When you're on the water, it's about 250 acres of private water in this famous mill pond. You own the whole [thing]. You own everything," explains listing agent Jon Kohler with Jon Kohler Associates.

“When you're out in a boat, you're not used to being the only one in this enormous lake. It probably takes five or six hours to go around the whole thing. It’s huge.”

“It's just fascinating. There's really nothing else like it, nor can there ever be again,” Kohler says. “You control it. You're the only boat that's able to duck hunt, fish, there.”

Cordray's Mill was once a resort property with a dance hall, swimming lake, and more, dating back to the early 1900s and continuing at least through the 1960s.

“There are a couple of remnant guest homes from the era when it was a resort, it was one of the first resorts in Georgia,” he says. “People would come from all over for events and whatnot.”

One structure is a converted church that is now a four bedroom and 1.5 bathroom house, in addition other living spaces.

“There's a house back where the mill pond house used to stand. There's a house on the lake, but back during its heyday, there were all types of structures, stores, shops, everything,” Kohler says.

The two houses have a total of six bedrooms and two bathrooms.

Another structure is a converted grain silo with one bedroom and a bathroom.

“It's one of those great ideas that not many people are able to pull off because the walls are rounded. It’s a really quaint little home.”

But the draw isn’t all of the places for people to stay, it’s about the activities.

“My favorite thing is the diversity of the habitats. You have almost every great habitat that Southwest Georgia has, but on one property,” Kohler explains, adding there is irrigated farmland and timberland in addition to the lake and waterfront.

The trophy whitetail deer make the property something special.

“I see it being owned by somebody that wants something that no one else has, which is the diversity and really the trophy deer hunting and the trophy deer habitat,” he says.

“Just a wildness factor that most places don't offer. [The perfect buyer is] somebody that really just wants to have the best of the best trophy whitetail, a lot of turkeys, and top it off with waterfowl, in an abundance greater than what most places offer.”

The property could also meet another need, a social storm property, which is a term Kohler trademarked several years ago.

“We recognized a trend that people were buying land for the lack of better word in case s**t hit the fan,” he said with a laugh, comparing the term to a prepper's property.

“This property is an ideal social storm property as well as a legacy sporting estate. I see most of these great places, but I had no idea a place like that existed. It's like a unicorn.

"You have 250 acres of water backed up in one of the largest private lakes in southeast United States all to yourself.”

Tiffani Sherman is an experienced freelance writer based in the Tampa, Florida area who covers real estate, personal finance, travel, and more for a variety of print and online publications. She works to support her travel habit and has been to all seven continents and about 100 countries. Tiffani is a proud Duke Blue Devil.

"I didn't really like him" - James Trafford's first thoughts on new West Ham boss Scott Parker

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Leeds United goalkeeper James Trafford has revealed his initial relationship with new West Ham United boss Scott Parker was difficult during their time together at Burnley.

The duo were part of a record-breaking Burnley side, with the club accumulating 100 points and keeping a record 30 clean sheets in the second tier. They conceded just 16 goals as they were promoted alongside Leeds United.

Now languishing around the relegation zone in the Championship, James Trafford has joined Leeds, while West Ham United have Scott Parker in charge. It's all change across the three clubs, highlighting just how quickly things can change in football.

Trafford has revealed that, despite Burnley's excellent form with him in goal, Parker did not see eye-to-eye with the goalkeeper. The 24-year-old spoke to the BBC and revealed his thoughts.

He said: "Yeah, I had a big one with Scott Parker. We had a very, very weird start to my time at Burnley with him. And I don't think he liked me too much.

"I didn't really like him too much. It was just weird. We didn't like, we couldn't connect, it was hard. But then, by the end of it, he understood me really, really well. And we got along really, really well."

He was asked: "How did you get to that point?"

Trafford added: "I don't know. I actually don't know."

The interviewer replied: "It probably helped that you were playing pretty well on the pitch, didn't it?"

Trafford concluded: "Probably. Yeah, but he really understood me."

Trafford's quirkiness wasn't for everyone but some enjoyed his company at Burnley, including Bristol City's Scott Twine. He spoke of their friendship at Turf Moor, adding: "Well, I mean, I have Pokemon cards. Love Pokemon cards. Oh, I love them."

The interviewer asked: "What do you trade them?"

He said: "No, just open the pack. Cool. Good card. Put them in my binder. Collecting all the sets.

"I've always done it. I remember I used to live with Scott Twine and we were so bored in a pre-season and we did loads of different things.

"And one of them was just go buy them. Everything had to be a competition as well, so we ended up getting big into tennis. That was one thing.

"We used to play card games against each other on our phones and then the other thing was Pokemon cards. We used to just open them. It's just something I've done since."

On his relationship with Lawrence Vigoroux and how that influenced him at Turf Moor, Trafford also said: "Obviously, there's a lot of other goalies that I have watched and really like parts of their game, like Lawrence Vigoroux. I really enjoyed working with him. He had some really good things.

"Then, obviously, everyone talks about [Manuel] Neuer, [Thibaut] Courtois, and like the big hitters, but also some goalies that haven't

played at that level that are still [big influences]. You can take bits from their game."

Burnley supporters may be surprised to hear that Trafford and Parker did not immediately click, given how successful their partnership ultimately became at Turf Moor.

Trafford was outstanding behind a side that set Championship records during the 2024/25 promotion campaign, while Parker's organisation helped produce one of the most formidable defensive units the second tier has seen.

Will Burnley survive this season? Comment your thoughts below!

FLW have ranked the 10 best EFL Championship stadiums for atmosphere so far in the 2026/27 season.

The contrast with the current side could hardly be greater. Burnley have gone from conceding just 16 goals across 46 Championship matches in 2024/25 to shipping 16 in their opening seven games of this season.

Their defensive performances have been particularly poor compared with the standards Trafford and Parker helped establish, with a lack of organisation, consistency, and reliability at the back exposing just how far the club have fallen from that record-breaking team.

Tottenham to submit formal complaint after controversial red card decision at Leeds

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Your weekly briefing on Tottenham, featuring team news and expert analysis from Sam Tabuteau

I would like to be emailed about offers, event and updates from Evening Standard. Read our privacy notice.

Tottenham are set to submit a formal complaint about the decision not to send off Jayden Bogle for his challenge on Mathys Tel during their 1-0 defeat to Leeds.

Tel had to come off shortly before half time after Bogle landed with his studs up on the Frenchman’s left Achilles at Elland Road.

Despite initially trying to play on, the forward soon signalled to come off and was later spotted with crutches and a protective boot on.

Roberto De Zerbi said in his post-match press conference that while further medical checks will take place, the initial fear is that Tel has suffered “a bad injury” and is likely to spend a considerable amount of time on the sidelines.

Standard Sport understands Tottenham believe Bogle, who received no punishment for the challenge despite a four-minute VAR review, should have been sent off and will write to the refereeing body, Pro Ref, to seek an explanation as to why Bogle was not dismissed.

While De Zerbi was reluctant to blame the decision for Spurs’ performance, the north London side lost control of the game after Tel’s withdrawal, with Noah Okafor eventually breaking the deadlock in the 79th minute.

On the incident, the head coach said he had full confidence in VAR despite venting his frustrations on the touchline and receiving a yellow card, which will now see him serve a touchline ban when Spurs return to action at home to Ipswich.

“No, I have big confidence in the referee,” De Zerbi said when asked if he would have wanted referee John Brooks to go to the monitor to review Bogle’s challenge.

“Nothing changes if he goes to watch the clip or not. I think there was the VAR, and if they didn’t take the right decision, I don’t know. But we have confidence, we have to keep our eyes on the performance where we can improve.”

U17 : On Aytone puis on bétonne

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Vous avez pu suivre cette rencontre en direct sur notre forum grâce à nos envoyés spéciaux Stéphanois et MachuPicchu42 : les U17 ont battu leurs homologues clermontois sur le plus petit des scores cet après-midi à Aimé-Jacquet.

Lors d'une première période guère emballante, seul l'ailier gauche Naël Dani a mis en danger des Auvergnats un peu plus entreprenants mais bien muselés par la charnière centrale Naoufan Mnemoi - Lucas Dagbo.

Juste après l'heure de jeu, sur une bonne passe du numéro 8 Sabri Sercu, Isaak Saydi a fait une percée supersonique avant de délivrer une passe décisive à Gibril Aytone, entré en jeu dès la 32e minute à la place de Léo Fayolle.

Les Verts ont beaucoup souffert pour conserver leur court mais très précieux avantage. Ils ont heureusement pu compter sur la maladresse des visiteurs, qui ont eu en outre la malchance de trouver la barre transversale d'Andrea Laugier à la 87e minute.

La compo : Laugier - Fayolle (Aytone, 33'), Mnemoi, Dagbo, Bufferne - Diakité (Giangreco, 61'), Sercu, Thibaudat - Seydi, Kimpioka, Dani (Tiambo, 90').

La réaction de David Le Moal : "En première mi-temps, on a eu quelques sorties de balle intéressantes, mais par contre il y a eu un manque de coordination et de connexion entre tous les joueurs de l'équipe. Je trouve qu'on a manqué d'énergie aussi. La seconde mi-temps a été encore plus laborieuse. On n'a fait quasiment que défendre après la pause. On a eu quelques contres sur lesquels on a mal joué les coups. On a beaucoup subi même en étant bien en place derrière. C'est dommage qu'on ait concédé autant d'occasions alors qu'on avait décidé de fermer un peu le jeu. Le résultat est bon, on a gagné. Mais en ce qui concerne le contenu de ce match, il n'y a pas grand chose de positif à retenir. On a décroché une victoire sans prendre de but avec beaucoup de U16 qui ne jouaient pas à leut poste donc ça fait quand même quelques satisfactions."

FacileRent Molinari Napoli, presentata la stagione 2026/27: sport, territorio e nuove ambizioni

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

– Passione, ambizione e vicinanza al territorio. All’insegna di questi aspetti la Molinari Napoli ha presentato il nuovo anno sportivo ed in particolare la stagione della squadra che competerà in Serie B2 femminile. Un evento – quello tenutosi sabato 7 novembre al PalaVesuvio – che ha coinvolto società, istituzioni, partner, staff tecnico e atlete, per raccontare obiettivi, ambizioni e prospettive di un progetto che vuole continuare a investire nello sport e nel territorio.

«Lavoriamo sul territorio da anni, avvicinando i giovani di Ponticelli allo sport. Da cinque anni sentiamo la vicinanza dell’Amministrazione comunale che ci ha aiutato in qualsiasi circostanza», ha dichiarato il Presidente della Molinari Napoli, Gennaro Nappo. «Per la Serie B2 c’è tanta ambizione, voglia di crescere e di essere d’esempio per il settore giovanile. Abbiamo costruito una squadra competitiva e speriamo che il campo ce lo dimostri».

Presenti alla conferenza l’On. Borrelli e l’Ass. Ferrante

«Io non credo più all’idea del centro e delle periferie per come si sta evolvendo il sistema sociale del nostro territorio. Ogni luogo come questo (il PalaVesuvio di Via Argine n.d.r.) è oro colato da valorizzare», ha dichiarato l’Onorevole Francesco Emilio Borrelli, presente alla conferenza stampa. «Lo sport è qualcosa di straordinario non solo perché ci insegna la disciplina, il rispetto del prossimo e l’impegno, ma anche perché ci spinge a fare attività fisica. Noi siamo in questo momento la Regione più obesa d’Italia per quanto riguarda i bambini. Bambini che non fanno sport: è importante fargli fare un’attività pulita e sana». Borrelli ha concluso il suo intervento sottolineando la vicinanza alla squadra: «Verrò sicuramente a vedere una delle vostre partite: tiferò per voi».

«Lo sport è una leva fondamentale, anche se a volte viene considerato secondario, soprattutto per quanto riguarda i supporti economici», ha dichiarato l’Assessora allo Sport ed alle Pari Opportunità del Comune di Napoli, Emanuela Ferrante. «Cinque anni fa, con grande difficoltà, ho provato a recuperare delle somme per riqualificare gli impianti, senza i quali non si può andare da nessuna parte. A conclusione di questo anno, che ha visto Napoli essere Capitale Europea dello Sport, posso dire con fierezza e con grande gioia che abbiamo investito 35 milioni di euro su tanti gli impianti sportivi. Partendo dagli impianti riqualificati si può ambire a realizzare quello che penso sia il diritto di tutti i ragazzi: fare sport».

L’Assessora si è rivolta poi direttamente alla squadra. «Siete una testimonianza ed un esempio per tutte le ragazze ed i ragazzi di Ponticelli. Questa stagione sarà una straordinaria esperienza e cercheremo di far riempire la palestra ogni volta che giocherete in casa».
